Q114Biology / both
subjectBiology
branchboth
chapterBiomolecules high confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on the definitions of enzyme cofactors (prosthetic group, apoenzyme vs holoenzyme), which are taught under enzymes and cofactors in Biomolecules.
subtopic 1Classification And Nomenclature Of Enzymes And Cofactors
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the definitions of enzyme cofactors -- prosthetic group, coenzyme/metal ion and apoenzyme versus holoenzyme -- which are taught under enzyme cofactors.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Consider the following statement : Select the correct option.
ACoenzyme or metal ion that is tightly bound to enzyme protein is called prosthetic group.
BA complete catalytic active enzyme with its bound prosthetic group is called apoenzyme.
  1. Both (A) and (B) are true.
  2. (A) is true but (B) is false.
  3. Both (A) and (B) are false.
  4. (A) is false but (B) is true.
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (3)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. Statement A: a cofactor that is tightly bound to the enzyme protein is called a prosthetic group, so A is true.
  2. Statement B: the protein part alone is the apoenzyme; apoenzyme + cofactor = holoenzyme, the complete catalytically active enzyme, so B is false.
  3. A true and B false matches option 2.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
32 highsolved differs from official
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsJudge statement A by recalling the definition of a prosthetic group (a cofactor tightly bound to the apoenzyme) → Judge statement B by recalling that the catalytically active enzyme with its bound cofactor is the holoenzyme, while the protein part alone is the apoenzyme → Combine the two true/false verdicts and pick the matching option
whyThe work is two separate definition recalls from the enzyme cofactor section, then putting the two verdicts together to choose the option; no calculation or insight is needed.
the catchStatement B swaps holoenzyme for apoenzyme, a common mix-up that leads straight to the 'both true' option.

solver reasoning: A tightly bound cofactor is a prosthetic group (A true); the complete catalytically active enzyme with its bound cofactor is the holoenzyme, not the apoenzyme (B false).

Q130Biology / zoology
subjectBiology
branchzoology
chapterBody Fluids and Circulation high confidence
why this chapterThe answer depends on cardiac output = stroke volume (end-diastolic minus end-systolic volume, 50 mL) x heart rate, giving 5000/50 = 100 beats per minute, which is taught under the cardiac cycle in Body Fluids and Circulation.
subtopic 1Cardiac Cycle
why this subtopicThe answer uses cardiac output = stroke volume (end-diastolic minus end-systolic volume, 100 - 50 = 50 mL) x heart rate, so 5000/50 = 100 beats per minute; stroke volume and cardiac output are taught under the cardiac cycle.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
What would be the heart rate of a person if the cardiac output is 5 L, blood volume in the ventricles at the end of diastole is 100 mL and at the end of ventricular systole is 50 mL?
  1. 50 beats per minute
  2. 75 beats per minute
  3. 100 beats per minute
  4. 125 beats per minute
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (3)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 3 · high
  1. Stroke volume is end-diastolic volume minus end-systolic volume: SV = 100 mL - 50 mL = 50 mL.
  2. Cardiac output (per minute) is 5 L = 5000 mL.
  3. CO = SV × HR ⇒ HR = COSV = 5000 mL/min50 mL = 100 beats per minute, which is option 3.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
33 highagree
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsRecall that stroke volume = end-diastolic volume minus end-systolic volume and compute it from 100 mL and 50 mL → Convert the cardiac output of 5 L to mL so units match the stroke volume → Recall cardiac output = stroke volume x heart rate and divide cardiac output by stroke volume to get beats per minute
whyThe work is recalling two NCERT relations (stroke volume as EDV minus ESV, and cardiac output as stroke volume times heart rate), a litre-to-millilitre conversion, and one division.
the catchOption 1 (50 beats per minute) is exactly the result of taking the end-diastolic volume of 100 mL as the stroke volume instead of subtracting the end-systolic volume.

Q132Biology / zoology
subjectBiology
branchzoology
chapterExcretory Products and Their Elimination high confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on the countercurrent mechanism keeping the inner medullary interstitium hyperosmolar so the filtrate can be concentrated, which is taught in Excretory Products and Their Elimination.
subtopic 1Mechanism of Concentration of the Filtrate
why this subtopicThe answer is that the countercurrent mechanism keeps the inner medullary interstitium hyperosmolar, which is what lets the kidney concentrate urine; this is taught under the mechanism of concentration of the filtrate.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Which of the following factors is responsible for the formation of concentrated urine?
  1. Low levels of antidiuretic hormone
  2. Maintaining hyperosmolarity towards inner medullary interstitium in the kidneys.
  3. Secretion of erythropoietin by Juxtaglomerular complex
  4. Hydrostatic pressure during glomerular filtration
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (2)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. Low ADH reduces water reabsorption in the DCT and collecting duct, so it gives dilute urine, not concentrated urine; option 1 is out.
  2. Erythropoietin from the JG cells stimulates RBC formation and has no role in concentrating urine; option 3 is out.
  3. Glomerular hydrostatic pressure drives filtration (GFR), not the concentration of the filtrate; option 4 is out.
  4. The counter-current mechanism of Henle's loop and the vasa recta keeps an increasing medullary interstitial osmolarity from 300 to about 1200 mOsmol L-1.
  5. This gradient draws water out of the collecting duct, so the urine becomes concentrated, which is option 2.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
22 highagree
difficultyeasy · 1 step
stepsRecall from NCERT (Excretory Products) that the counter-current mechanism maintains an increasing osmolarity gradient in the inner medullary interstitium, which concentrates urine, and match that to the option stating it
whyThe work is a single recall of the NCERT statement linking the medullary osmotic gradient to urine concentration; the other options (low ADH, erythropoietin, filtration pressure) are dismissed by the same recalled fact.

printed in the booklet

Which of the following ecological pyramids is generally inverted?
  1. Pyramid of numbers in grassland
  2. Pyramid of energy
  3. Pyramid of biomass in a forest
  4. Pyramid of biomass in a sea
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (4)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
  1. A pyramid of energy is always upright, because only about 10% of energy passes to each next trophic level. That rules out option 2.
  2. In a grassland the pyramid of numbers is upright (many grasses > herbivores > carnivores), and in a forest the pyramid of biomass is upright. That rules out options 1 and 3.
  3. In the sea, the biomass of phytoplankton is smaller than the biomass of the fish that eat them, so the pyramid of biomass is inverted. The answer is option 4.

subtopic 2Natural Contraceptive Methods

READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader

Q151Biology / zoology
subjectBiology
branchzoology
chapterEvolution high confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on the Hardy-Weinberg equation p2 + 2pq + q2 = 1 (p = 0.4, q = 0.6 gives 0.16 AA, 0.48 Aa, 0.36 aa), which is taught under the Hardy-Weinberg Principle in Evolution.
subtopic 1Hardy-Weinberg Principle
why this subtopicThe answer rests on the Hardy-Weinberg equation p2 + 2pq + q2 = 1: with p = 0.4 and q = 0.6 it gives AA = 0.16, Aa = 0.48 and aa = 0.36.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
A gene locus has two alleles A, a. If the frequency of dominant allele A is 0.4, then what will be the frequency of homozygous dominant, heterozygous and homozygous recessive individuals in the population?
  1. 0.36(AA); 0.48(Aa); 0.16(aa)
  2. 0.16(AA); 0.24(Aa); 0.36(aa)
  3. 0.16(AA); 0.48(Aa); 0.36(aa)
  4. 0.16(AA); 0.36(Aa); 0.48(aa)
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (3)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 3 · high
  1. Allele frequencies sum to one: q = 1 - p = 1 - 0.4 = 0.6.
  2. Homozygous dominant: p2 = (0.4)2 = 0.16.
  3. Heterozygous: 2pq = 2 × 0.4 × 0.6 = 0.48.
  4. Homozygous recessive: q2 = (0.6)2 = 0.36.
  5. 0.16(AA); 0.48(Aa); 0.36(aa) matches option 3.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
33 highagree
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsRecall Hardy-Weinberg relation p + q = 1 and compute q (frequency of a) = 1 - 0.4 → Apply p2 + 2pq + q2 = 1 to compute p2 (AA), 2pq (Aa) and q2 (aa) → Match the three computed genotype frequencies, in the AA/Aa/aa order, against the options
whyThe work is recalling the Hardy-Weinberg equation, deriving the recessive allele frequency, and substituting both allele frequencies into the three genotype terms.
the catchDistractors match plausible slips: one swaps the AA and aa values (using p for q), one uses pq instead of 2pq for heterozygotes, one shuffles the values across genotypes.

  1. (a) (iii) (b) (ii) (c) (i) (d) (iv)

Q163Biology / zoology
subjectBiology
branchzoology
chapterExcretory Products and Their Elimination medium confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on what haemodialysis (artificial kidney, taught under disorders of the excretory system) cannot replace: the kidney's non-excretory roles behind calcium absorption and RBC production, while it does remove nitrogenous waste and excess K+.
subtopic 1Disorders Of The Excretory System
why this subtopicThe answer depends on what hemodialysis (artificial kidney), taught under disorders of the excretory system, cannot replace: the kidney's role in calcium absorption (vitamin D activation) and RBC production (erythropoietin).
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Use of an artificial kidney during hemodialysis may result in :
Which of the following options is the most appropriate?
aNitrogenous waste build-up in the body
bNon-elimination of excess potassium ions
cReduced absorption of calcium ions from gastro-intestinal tract
dReduced RBC production
  1. (a) and (b) are correct
  2. (b) and (c) are correct
  3. (c) and (d) are correct
  4. (a) and (d) are correct
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (3)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 3 · medium
  1. Haemodialysis filters the blood through the dialysing fluid, removing nitrogenous wastes such as urea, so (a) nitrogenous waste build-up is not expected.
  2. The dialysing fluid matches plasma except for wastes, so excess K+ ions are also removed; (b) is not expected.
  3. The artificial kidney does not make calcitriol (active vitamin D), so absorption of Ca2+ from the gastro-intestinal tract falls; (c) is correct.
  4. The artificial kidney does not secrete erythropoietin, so RBC production falls; (d) is correct.
  5. (c) and (d) are correct, which is option 3.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
33 mediumagree
difficultyhard · 4 steps
stepsRecall what a hemodialysis unit does: it filters blood across a membrane against dialysing fluid, removing urea/nitrogenous wastes and excess ions such as K+, so statements (a) and (b) describe things dialysis prevents rather than causes → Recognise the non-obvious premise that the question is about what the artificial kidney cannot replace: the endocrine functions of the failed kidney → Recall that the kidney secretes erythropoietin (Chemical Coordination chapter), so its absence in a dialysis-dependent patient leads to reduced RBC production - evaluate (d) → Recall that the kidney converts vitamin D to its active form (calcitriol), needed for Ca2+ absorption from the gut, so its absence reduces calcium absorption - evaluate (c), then match the surviving pair to the options
whyThe work is ruling out the two excretory statements dialysis actually handles and then linking two separate endocrine roles of the kidney (erythropoietin and vitamin D activation) to the patient's condition, drawing on the excretion and hormone chapters together.
the catchThe stem says the artificial kidney 'may result in', which invites reading (a) and (b) as plausible failures of dialysis - option (1) is the exact result of that wrong reading; the vitamin D/calcitriol role of the kidney is barely stated in NCERT, and the key insight (dialysis does not replace the kidney's hormonal functions) is never stated in the stem.

Q164Biology / zoology
subjectBiology
branchzoology
chapterNeural Control and Coordination high confidence
why this chapterThe answer depends on the structure of the cornea as the transparent anterior part of the eye-ball's outer layer, which is taught under the eye as a sense organ in Neural Control and Coordination.
subtopic 1Sense Organs: The Eye
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the structure of the cornea as the external transparent protective covering of the eyeball, which is taught under the eye as a sense organ.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Which of the following statements is correct?
  1. Cornea is an external, transparent and protective proteinacious covering of the eye-ball.
  2. Cornea consists of dense connective tissue of elastin and can repair itself.
  3. Cornea is convex, transparent layer which is highly vascularised.
  4. Cornea consists of dense matrix of collagen and is the most sensitive portion the eye.
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (1)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · medium
  1. Option 3 is false: the cornea is transparent because it is avascular, not highly vascularised.
  2. Option 2 is false: the corneal stroma is dense connective tissue of collagen, not elastin.
  3. Option 1 is false: the cornea is the transparent front part of the outer coat, not a protective proteinaceous covering of the whole eyeball.
  4. Option 4 is true: the cornea is a dense collagen matrix with the densest nerve supply, so it is the most sensitive part of the eye. The answer is option 4.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
14 mediumsolved differs from official
difficultyhard · 4 steps
stepsJudge statement 1: whether the cornea is an external protective covering described as 'proteinaceous' (it is the transparent anterior part of the fibrous sclera layer) → Judge statement 2: whether the corneal stroma is elastin-based dense connective tissue and whether it repairs itself → Judge statement 3: whether the cornea is vascularised (it is avascular, which is why it is transparent and used in grafts) → Judge statement 4: whether the cornea is a dense collagen matrix and the most sensitive part of the eye, then pick the one statement that survives
whyEach of the four options makes a separate histological claim about the cornea (protein covering, elastin vs collagen, vascularity, sensitivity), so the candidate must recall and test four distinct facts and eliminate three.
the catchThe facts tested (collagen matrix, avascularity, highest sensory sensitivity, self-repair) go beyond the one-line NCERT description of the cornea as the anterior transparent part of the sclera, and the eye section has been dropped from the rationalised NCERT Class 11 syllabus; option 1 is a loosely worded near-true statement that competes with the intended one.

solver reasoning: The cornea is a dense, transparent, avascular collagen matrix and is the most sensitive part of the eye; it is not vascularised, not an elastin tissue, and not just a proteinaceous outer covering.

Q172Biology / both
subjectBiology
branchboth
chapterMolecular Basis of Inheritance high confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on the triplet nature of the genetic code: deleting three consecutive bases (GGU) removes one codon without shifting the reading frame, a frameshift-mutation concept taught under the genetic code.
subtopic 1Effect of Mutation on Genetic Code
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the fact that deleting or inserting three bases (one whole codon) does not shift the reading frame, while one or two base changes cause a frameshift, which is taught under the effect of mutation on the genetic code.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Under which of the following conditions will there be no change in the reading frame of following mRNA? 5'AACAGCGGUGCUAUU3'
  1. Insertion of G at 5th position
  2. Deletion of G from 5th position
  3. Insertion of A and G at 4th and 5th positions respectively
  4. Deletion of GGU from 7th, 8th and 9th positions
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (4)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
  1. Split the mRNA into triplets from the 5' end: AAC | AGC | GGU | GCU | AUU.
  2. Positions 7,8,9 are G,G,U, which is exactly the third codon GGU.
  3. Deleting GGU removes 3 bases (one whole codon): AAC | AGC | GCU | AUU, so the reading frame of the rest is unchanged.
  4. Inserting or deleting 1 base (options 1, 2) or inserting 2 bases (option 3) changes the number of bases by a non-multiple of 3, causing a frameshift.
  5. Hence the reading frame is unchanged only in option 4.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
44 highagree
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsRecall that the reading frame is read in non-overlapping triplets, so only an insertion or deletion of a multiple of three bases leaves the downstream frame intact → Count the bases added or removed in each option (1 inserted, 1 deleted, 2 inserted, 3 deleted) and keep only the change that is a multiple of three → Check the sequence by position to confirm the bases named in that option actually sit at the stated positions and form one complete codon in the frame
whyThe candidate recalls the triplet/frameshift rule from the NCERT genetic code section, then applies it by counting the bases changed in each option and checking the positions against the given sequence.
page 19 · printed answer 4 · open source PDF at this page · NEET_2019_Code_P1_official.pdf

Q179Biology / zoology
subjectBiology
branchzoology
chapterChemical Coordination and Integration high confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on which disorder results from each hormone's imbalance (insulin-diabetes mellitus, thyroxin-goitre, corticoids-Addison's disease, growth hormone-acromegaly), which is taught under endocrine glands and disorders.
subtopic 1Endocrine Disorders
why this subtopicThe answer depends on knowing the disease caused by each hormone's imbalance (insulin-diabetes mellitus, thyroxin-goitre, corticoids-Addison's disease, growth hormone-acromegaly), which is the subject of endocrine disorders.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Match the following hormones with the respective disease
Select the correct option.
(a)Insulin(i)Addison's disease
(b)Thyroxin(ii)Diabetes insipidus
(c)Corticoids(iii)Acromegaly
(d)Growth Hormone(iv)Goitre
(v)Diabetes mellitus
  1. (a)-(v), (b)-(i), (c)-(ii), (d)-(iii)
  2. (a)-(ii), (b)-(iv), (c)-(iii), (d)-(i)
  3. (a)-(v), (b)-(iv), (c)-(i), (d)-(iii)
  4. (a)-(ii), (b)-(iv), (c)-(i), (d)-(iii)
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (3)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 3 · high
  1. Insulin deficiency or resistance gives hyperglycaemia, i.e. diabetes mellitus: (a) → (v).
  2. Thyroxin synthesis falls in iodine deficiency, enlarging the thyroid as goitre: (b) → (iv).
  3. Deficient secretion of adrenal corticoids causes Addison's disease: (c) → (i).
  4. Excess growth hormone in adults causes acromegaly: (d) → (iii).
  5. The match (a)-(v), (b)-(iv), (c)-(i), (d)-(iii) is option 3.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
33 highagree
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsRecall that insulin deficiency causes diabetes mellitus (not diabetes insipidus, which is ADH deficiency) - eliminates options 2 and 4 → Recall that thyroxin (iodine) deficiency is linked to goitre, while Addison's disease is due to corticoid hyposecretion - eliminates option 1 → Confirm the remaining pairing: corticoids with Addison's disease and growth hormone excess with acromegaly
whyThe work is recalling four hormone-to-disorder associations from the NCERT endocrine chapter and using two or three of them to eliminate options in the match grid.
the catchDiabetes insipidus is a distractor for insulin; a candidate who links any 'diabetes' to insulin, forgetting it is caused by ADH deficiency, picks a wrong option.

Q180Biology / zoology
subjectBiology
branchzoology
chapterLocomotion and Movement high confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on the classification of the 12 pairs of ribs into 7 vertebrosternal, 3 vertebrochondral and 2 vertebral (floating) pairs, which is part of the axial skeleton in Locomotion and Movement.
subtopic 1Axial Skeleton
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the classification of the 12 pairs of ribs into 7 vertebrosternal, 3 vertebrochondral and 2 vertebral (floating) pairs, which is taught under the axial skeleton (rib cage).
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Select the correct option.
  1. 8th, 9th and 10th pairs of ribs articulate directly with the sternum.
  2. 11th and 12th pairs of ribs are connected to the sternum with the help of hyaline cartilage.
  3. Each rib is a flat thin bone and all the ribs are connected dorsally to the thoracic vertebrae and ventrally to the sternum.
  4. There are seven pairs of vertebrosternal, three pairs of vertebrochondral and two pairs of vertebral ribs.
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (4)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
  1. The first 7 pairs of ribs attach ventrally to the sternum through hyaline cartilage, so they are true (vertebrosternal) ribs.
  2. Pairs 8, 9 and 10 do not reach the sternum directly; they join the hyaline cartilage of the 7th rib, so they are vertebrochondral (false) ribs, and option 1 is false.
  3. Pairs 11 and 12 are not connected ventrally at all; they are floating (vertebral) ribs, so options 2 and 3 are false.
  4. 7 + 3 + 2 = 12 pairs: 7 vertebrosternal, 3 vertebrochondral, 2 vertebral, which is option 4.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
44 highagree
difficultyeasy · 1 step
stepsRecall the NCERT classification of the 12 rib pairs (1st-7th vertebrosternal attached directly to the sternum, 8th-10th vertebrochondral joined via the 7th rib's hyaline cartilage, 11th-12th floating with no ventral connection) and check each option against it
whyThe work is a single recall of the rib-type counts and attachments from the Locomotion and Movement chapter, which each option either repeats or misstates.
page 20 · printed answer 4 · open source PDF at this page · NEET_2019_Code_P1_official.pdf
